# Reasoning-strength evidence: the verified claim sets behind the matrix and request v6

**Status:** a record, frozen on 27 September 2026. This file holds every claim the two research workflows returned after a skeptic re-opened its source, so that nothing behind the [reasoning-strength matrix](reasoning-level-matrix.md) lives only in a manager session (OD-31). Each claim carries its source and the quote relied on; the confidence is the skeptic's: *verified* (the quote was read on the page), *inferred* (arithmetic or comparison on quoted numbers) or *unverified* (no page found; a gap, not a fact). Vendor pages are labelled where they were the only source. Corrections the skeptics made are kept in the claim text.

The first workflow (sections 1 to 5) researched Anthropic's documentation and the Claude API skill; the second (sections 6 to 10) researched third-party benchmarks and use cases, which the request v6 rests on. Coverage tables say, per model and level, what independent evidence exists.

## 1. Claude Fable 5.1 (claude-fable-5-1) effort levels: low, medium, high, xhigh, max - behaviour, recommendations, cost, comparisons, Claude Code handling, refusal classifiers (skeptic re-check of researcher claim set)

| 12 | [verified] Long deliverables at xhigh/max: the model may draft the deliverable in thinking and write it again as the reply (longer wait, roughly double output tokens); fix is to run at high, or at xhigh/max set max_tokens with room for thinking plus reply and append the anti-double-drafting note to the end of the user message, leaving earlier copies in place byte-for-byte on later requests. |  | /tmp/claude-0/bundled-skills/2.1.283/271002a193cc138e5028a9cf73d731d3/claude-api/shared/model-migration.md:1760-1763 | At `xhigh`, and especially `max`, the model thinks more before it starts writing. When one request asks for a long deliverable ... it may draft much of it in its thinking and then write it out again as the reply: a longer wait and roughly double the output tokens. Simplest fix: run those requests at `high` ... If you do run them at `xhigh`/`max`, set `max_tokens` to leave room for the thinking *and* the reply, and append this to the end of the user message | verified |

| 19 | [verified] Batching nudge wording from the public docs; the public page also describes the one-call-per-turn behaviour in coding/computer-use loops where calls are implied, costing tokens, round trips and wall-clock but not answer quality. |  | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-fable-5-1 (section 'Batch independent tool calls in agent loops') | The exception is coding and computer-use loops where the next independent calls are implied by the task rather than explicitly requested ... there it may issue them one per turn instead. On Claude Fable 5.1 prefer this form over changing the top-level value between requests: a top-level change restarts the cache *and* steers the model less reliably (its earlier replies were written at the previous level and it tends to stay consistent with them) - though a top-level change does not invalidate thinking blocks. Unsupported models, Claude Fable 5 included, 400 | verified |

**Gaps**

- All seven web pages were fetched successfully in this container with curl through the proxy (HTTP 200) and checked against raw page text (platform.claude.com returned markdown directly), so the three announcement claims previously marked 'inferred' are now verified against the live page text; network was not blocked. WebFetch was not needed.
- No source gives per-level quantitative cost or accuracy figures for Fable 5.1 specifically. All measured effort-curve and cost-per-task numbers in cost-optimization.md are for Fable 5 (and Opus 5); the announcement gives only indexed aggregate cost (25%/45%) at default effort and one third-party benchmark score (CursorBench 73.4% at max). No dollar-per-task comparison of Fable 5.1 at low versus Opus 5.5 was found.
- The migration doc statement (model-migration.md:1466) that Fable 5.1 low 'often exceeds the xhigh or even max performance of previous models' was not found verbatim on any live public page fetched; the public effort page makes the analogous statement only for Fable 5 ('often exceed xhigh performance on prior models'), and the public prompting page says Fable 5.1 at low is 'often competitive with Claude Opus and Claude Sonnet models on cost per task while scoring higher'.
- The rationale for keeping the word 'privately' in the batching nudge (model reply to the reminder) appears only in the local migration doc (line 1765); the public prompting page gives the nudge text without that rationale.
- Claude Code's settings-reference (modelSettings, effortLevel, maxEffortLevel schemas), env-vars page and the Fable 5.1 'What's new' page were not fetched; details beyond the model-config page summary remain unverified.
- 'Fable 5.1 defaults to High in Claude Code, Medium in Cowork and Claude.ai' is supported only by the Anthropic announcement page; the code.claude.com model-config page confirms the Claude Code default of high generically ('high on every model that supports effort, except Opus 5.5 ... Opus 4.7') but no fetched page states Cowork or Claude.ai defaults.
- Claude Code page text about Fable consent prompts, alias resolution and fallback routing describes Claude Code behaviour as of the fetched page on 2026-09-27; version-gated statements (v2.1.257+, v2.1.219+) may change.
- The researcher's original line reference 1766 for the batching paragraph was wrong (blank line); corrected to 1765 (paragraph) and 1767 (nudge text). The researcher's citation of line 1742 for the 'consumer surfaces ship Opus 4.8 fallbacks' sentence was wrong; that sentence is at line 1367.

On multi-app automation tasks, an 'explore broadly before acting' instruction improved Opus 5.5 completion at both medium and max effort, at the cost of slightly more tool calls and tokens. |  | https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-opus-5-5 (Explore context in multi-app workflows) | In Anthropic's testing on multi-app automation tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 completed noticeably more of them correctly with this instruction, at both `medium` and `max` effort, at the cost of slightly more tool calls and tokens. | verified |
| 47 | NEW. **Gaps**

- No source, local or live, gives a per-level (low/medium/high/xhigh/max) description written specifically for Claude Opus 5.5. The live effort page's Opus 5.5 section names only medium (default), refers to 'the higher levels' for max_tokens, and defers to the generic table; Opus-5.5-specific text elsewhere covers low (TTFT; close to Opus 5 high on several coding evals), medium (default; matches or exceeds Opus 5 high), and xhigh/max as a pair (more thinking per turn, longer turns, reserve for measured gains). Nothing Opus-5.5-specific was found about 'high'.
- Several quantitative figures exist only in the local bundled skill (model-migration.md, written pre-launch) and are absent from the live launch pages: 'about half the tokens' for agentic coding (2034), 'roughly 40% fewer output tokens' for knowledge work (2035), 'roughly a tenth of the output tokens per chart' and 'roughly 40% fewer tokens' for computer use (2037). Live wording is 'fewer tokens' / 'a small fraction of the output tokens'. Treat the exact percentages as unverified against live docs.
- Measured effort-curve dollar/pass-rate figures in cost-optimization.md (lines 162-164) are for Claude Opus 5 and Claude Fable 5, not Opus 5.5; model-migration.md:2032 says to re-baseline them. The live whats-new page points to https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/optimizing-for-cost-and-intelligence#tune-effort for measured effort results, which was not fetched; no Opus 5.5 cost-per-task dollar figures were found.
- Fast-mode pricing for Opus 5.5 ($8 / $40 per MTok) is stated only in the local guide with 'confirm before quoting'; the live whats-new page confirms Claude API only and the fast-mode-2026-02-01 beta header but defers pricing to the Fast mode page, which was not fetched.
- Rate-limit pool for Opus 5.5 (separate vs shared with Opus 5) is unconfirmed: local guide says 'confirm at launch' and none of the live pages fetched addresses it.
- The local guide's 'exceeds' (2007) is stronger than the live 'matches or exceeds'; and the local code-review claim omits the live page's 'early testers' attribution. The live migration guide (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/opus-5-5/migration-guide) was not fetched, so its exact wording on these points is unverified.
- WebFetch succeeded for all five live URLs (effort, prompting-claude-opus-5-5, opus-5-5/overview, models/overview, whats-new-opus-5-5). WebSearch was not used. https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview was served as https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/models/overview.

**Gaps**

- The Workflow tool description quoted by the researcher ('Workflows can spawn dozens of agents ... default workflow size guideline: medium') remains unverified: the Workflow tool is not loaded in this subagent either (not in the deferred tool list). The workflow-authoring skill confirms such a description exists and the docs confirm the medium default, but the exact wording is unchecked.
- No official page gives a numeric token multiplier for ultracode versus a single session; the docs say 'meaningfully more tokens' and 'reaches a session or weekly limit sooner'. The 25-agent / 1.5M-token Large workflow thresholds are the only official numbers; the 7x figure applies to agent teams in plan mode only.
- Whether the ultracode keyword raises the single turn's effort to xhigh is not stated; workflows.md line 117 says the keyword runs the task as a workflow 'without changing the session's effort level'. Best statement: keyword = workflow only; session setting = workflow + xhigh.
- Whether a workflow agent with agentType naming a custom subagent inherits that subagent's frontmatter effort is not documented on the pages fetched (workflows.md, sub-agents.md).
- In the repository no record shows a session actually run with ultracode on; the I1 review's actually-used level is not confirmed in the files read (handoff table row 'Review of I1' shows 'pending').
- The stacknotice blog's statement that ultracode is 'not a prompt keyword' contradicts the official docs; the blogs should not be relied on beyond corroboration.
- /docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#workflow and /docs/en/agents were not fetched; settings-reference.md was fetched in addition to the researcher's sources.
- WebFetch and curl through the proxy both worked; no network blocks were encountered.

**Gaps**

- No source provides a direct level-for-level Fable 5.1 vs Opus 5.5 comparison of thinking amount or quality; the only direct cross-model guidance is the overview's 'start with Opus 5.5 ... use Fable 5.1 ... when your evals on Claude Opus 5.5 at higher effort still fall short'. Any cell-to-cell mapping is a chain of predecessor-relative inferences, not a documented result.
- No measured cost-per-task figure for Fable 5.1 vs Opus 5.5 exists in the sources; the only Fable-vs-Opus number is previous-generation (Opus 5 at ~60% of Fable 5's cost at equal pass rate), and both successors are said to be cheaper per task than their predecessors, so the ratio must be re-baselined, not scaled.
- The researcher's claim that the skill's quick reference generalises 'xhigh ... default in Claude Code' to Fable 5 / Opus 4.7/4.8 / Sonnet 5 could not be verified: no SKILL.md exists on disk under the claude-api skill directory and grep finds the wording only in the Opus 4.7 ladder (model-migration.md line 633). Whether Claude Code currently defaults Fable 5.1 or Opus 5.5 sessions to xhigh is unverified; the public effort page says only that Sonnet 5 'defaults to high effort on the Claude API and Claude Code'.
- The researcher's 'cached as of 2026-06-24' date for the local catalog is not present in any skill file (grep); the catalog's cache date is unverified.
- Opus 5.5 ZDR / data-retention status is explicitly left open in the local guide ('launch docs say nothing new - treat as Claude Opus 5'); not confirmed against live docs. The effort page's featureMetadata says ZDR eligibility 'Excludes Covered Models', consistent with Fable 5.1 being excluded, but does not name Opus 5.5 either way.
- No source states a refusal-rate difference between Fable 5.1 and Opus 5.5 for security review; the only textual asymmetry is that Fable 5.1's guide says it 'is not intended for those domains' (line 1367).
- 'max is uncapped' is stated explicitly only for Opus 5.5 (line 2007) and generically on the effort page ('no constraints on token spending', listing both models); no Fable 5.1-specific 'uncapped' wording found.
- Linked pages not fetched: 'Prompting Claude Fable 5.1', 'Prompting Claude Opus 5.5', 'Choosing a model', the per-model overview pages and the Opus 5.5 migration guide; they may contain more direct comparisons. WebFetch succeeded for both cited URLs; WebSearch was not attempted.

**Gaps**

- WebFetch and WebSearch were not attempted: every cited source was a local file or the Notion page, which the Notion connector fetched successfully on 27 Sep 2026 (page_last_edited_at 2026-09-27T01:38:17Z). The Notion uses database rows were not re-queried; row claims rest on the scratchpad export uses-rows.json (20 rows).
- No per-session cost, token usage or wall-clock duration exists in any row or repository document; only TypeSafe request tokens and latencies, two price ratios (Opus about 40% of Fable per token; Fable about 2.5x) and tooling durations (CI 6–11 min; fix reversals about 12 or about 15 min) are recorded. Any per-session cost statement would be unverified.
- The premise that two sessions have run on each model is only partly supported: completed rows show one Opus 5.5 session (I2a) and two Fable 5.1 sessions (I2a review, I2b); App Manager 3 on Opus 5.5 at max and DM-05 on Fable 5.1 at extra high have no outcome row.
- The I2b implementation row (row 18) does not say whether the session used sub-agents; explicit 'no sub-agents' statements exist only for the C2 review and I2a review rows, and the I1 review row says it did use one.
- Ultracode has never been run in this project, so its flag is untested in both directions; 'too low' and 'too high' have never been recorded, so nothing in the log can calibrate a level threshold.
- Whether the I2b revision 1 to revision 2 shape shift is caused by description length rather than the added content is an interpretation; the sources record only the rewording and the probability change.
- The researcher's reading of why m1 gave the I2b review to Opus (phrasing pulling toward the 'named fixes' criterion) is unsupported by any source statement; the row records only the probability and the manager's counter-argument.

ARC Prize: ARC-AGI-1 88.5%, ARC-AGI-2 70.1% (no cost). Vendor-only (Anthropic charts via aicatchup): TB4 38.5% $1.29, FrontierCode 47.3% $0.40, CursorBench 43.7% $1.18, AutomationBench 23.3% $0.50; Deloitte 72% bug catch at lowest effort (launch quote). No independent SWE-bench, HLE, SciCode, TB4, Vals, Arena, METR or LiveCodeBench at low. |

**Gaps**

- Per-level independent coding-agent measurements (SWE-bench Verified/Pro, tau-bench, LiveCodeBench, Terminal-Bench) exist for neither model at any level except AA's TB4 at xhigh/max for Opus 5.5 and Vals at max. Every full five-level TB4/FrontierCode/CursorBench/AutomationBench curve is Anthropic's own chart.
- Independent Terminal-Bench 4.0 disagrees by harness: AA 59.6% (xhigh = max), Vals 61.62% (max, with 30/198 fallback attempts; 53.54% if failed), tbench.ai public board has no Opus 5.5 row (Fable 5.1 57.9% there vs 49.49% at Vals). Effort for the tbench.ai Fable 5.1 run is not stated.
- Max vs xhigh, independent: Opus 5.5 gains +2 AA index points (1.73x cost, 2.6x tokens), +1.9 SciCode, +42 Briefcase Elo, +26 GDPval Elo, 0 on TB4, and loses 0.8 on ARC-AGI-2. Fable 5.1 gains 0 AA index points (1.28x cost, 1.58x tokens), +0.4 HLE, +9 Briefcase, +14 GDPval, +1.0 ARC-AGI-1, 0 on ARC-AGI-2. No independent cost-per-task for max vs xhigh outside AA and ARC (ARC gives cost only for Fable 5.1 max and Fable 5).
- AA rescored its index (v4.3.2) between 1 and 22 Sept 2026: Fable 5.1 max moved 66 -> 53 and $3.76 -> $7.63/task (190M vs 143.7M tokens), so AA article numbers for Fable 5.1 are not comparable with Opus 5.5's.
- Multi-agent / ultracode: the only controlled comparison (Dealwatch, n=10 runs, two repos) found orchestration neither helped nor hurt Opus 5.5 and that no worker was actually delegated to; it does not test ultracode itself, agent teams at scale, or any level other than high. No measured ultracode run exists on Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1. Token multipliers (7x, 41x, ten agents = 10x quota) are model-agnostic or older-model figures; the 7x figure could not be sourced at all.
- The researcher's emergent.sh claim mixed sources: rho and detection rates are from arXiv 2511.16708 (Sonnet 4.5 patches), and the Vals per-benchmark costs, the llm-stats 'unmaintained' statement, the SEAL 61.5% figure, and the morphllm ultracode quote could not be found on the cited pages.
- Arena: WebDev board page did not load; WebDev figures rest on remio's secondary report. Text Arena lists Opus 5.5 at High (1509), not max; Agent Arena has Fable 5.1 (Max) but no Opus 5.5 row yet.
- METR: no Fable 5.1 predeployment summary found; Opus 5.5 summary has no time horizon, effort, tokens or cost. Epoch: Opus 5.5 ECI N/A; Fable 5.1 effort unstated.
- Wall-clock per task by effort: only AA TTFT (Opus 5.5 6.1/13.0/39.1/144/n-a s; Fable 5.1 6.5/7.5/11.7/118/233 s) and CodeRabbit's Fable 5.1 low 18:38 vs high 21:36. Endor Labs' 2.2 vs 7.2 median minutes and Snorkel's 36% wall-clock figure do not state effort.
- Fable 5.1 medium and xhigh have no independent evidence beyond AA and ARC Prize plus one qualitative practitioner writeup; Opus 5.5 low and xhigh have none beyond AA and ARC Prize.
- Practitioner cost anecdotes (MindStudio $1,200 vs $500, joinoasis $8 vs $2, paddo.dev 16-minute window) are single runs with unstated effort and high variance (Firecrawl measured a 45% run-to-run spread on identical prompts).

## 7. max-in-practice (skeptic re-verification): what max effort does in practice on Claude Opus 5.5 and Claude Fable 5.1 vs xhigh and lower levels; third-party measurements, practitioner reports, code-review/security runs, token/time multipliers, overthinking, multi-agent (ultracode/ultrareview/agent teams). Each claim is prefixed VERIFIED / CORRECTED / REFUTED / NEW.

**Gaps**

- Corrected headline: there is NO independent benchmark in this set where Opus 5.5 max beats xhigh. The researcher's ARC-AGI-2 'max 97.5% vs xhigh 92.5%' was a transcription error (max is 91.7%); on ARC max trails high on both v1 and v2, and on Anthropic's own Terminal-Bench 4.0 and FrontierCode charts max trails xhigh/medium. AA's Index (+2) and Cursor's CursorBench 4.0 (+1.8) are the only places max edges xhigh, at 1.7-1.9x cost.
- For Fable 5.1, independent per-level evidence shows max = xhigh on AA Index v4.3.2 and ARC-AGI-2, +1.0 on ARC-AGI-1, +0.2 on CursorBench 4.0; only Anthropic's own Terminal-Bench 4.0 chart (+4.5) shows a material max gain, and it is vendor-run.
- No third-party code-review, security-audit or vulnerability-detection run at pure API max or pure xhigh with recall/precision/cost exists for either model. CodeRabbit's Opus 5.5 'Standard/Max' are mixed-effort pipeline configurations; CodeRabbit's Fable 5.1 test stops at High; depthfirst's Opus 5.5 figure is at high and secondhand; Endor Labs and Sonar do not state effort.
- No measured ultracode (dynamic workflow) run with tokens, dollars or minutes is attributable to Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1. The one measured run found (471,480 tokens, 264 s, 5 agents, 84 tool calls) names no model version; ultracode model lists differ between sources (Developers Digest omits both target models; claudefa.st includes Opus 5.5 but not Fable 5.1). Ultracode sends xhigh, not max, to the model.
- No agent-team or multi-agent-review study measures bugs found vs single-agent for either model at max; Dealwatch (high effort) is the only controlled orchestration benchmark and found orchestration added cost without quality gain.
- Artificial Analysis' Fable 5.1 numbers exist under two index versions (launch article 65/66 at $2.72/$3.76 vs v4.3.2 page 53/53 at $5.98/$7.63); they must not be mixed. AA does not state the effort level of its independent 59.6% Terminal-Bench 4.0 run for Opus 5.5.
- CursorBench 4.0 Opus 5.5 max cost is $13.43 in the reproduced Cursor table vs '$8-9' in a system-card summary relayed elsewhere; Cursor's own X post could not be fetched (HTTP 402) so the primary is unconfirmed.
- Vals AI runs both models only at max (plus Terminal-Bench 2.1 at high), so Vals gives no xhigh comparison; the Fable 5.1 Vals headline moved from 67.87% (1 Sept note) to 68.83% ±1.08 (current), and Opus 5.5 (69.69% ±0.94) leads within combined error.
- Two potentially relevant practitioner sources remain unreadable from this container: the Medium 'all 5 levels actually cost' post (HTTP 403) and the X trending quota-burn summary (HTTP 402); Reddit threads were not read directly.
- ARC Prize per-effort cost per task for Opus 5.5 is not on its results page; Fable 5.1 costs are given only for max.
- All predecessor results (Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Fable 5, Opus 4.7/4.8) are labelled as such; the preregistered arXiv 2608.01347 study has no max arm and does not cover the target models.
- Vendor guidance about max ('reserve for frontier problems', 'prone to overthinking', 'thinks more per turn at xhigh and max') is Anthropic's own text, cited here only as secondary context.

**Gaps**

- Drammeh incident-response paper (348 trials, 100% vs 1.7%) is withdrawn by its author (multi-agent arm was a hard-coded constant); the FlowHunt summary and the researcher's claim must be dropped.
- Both ARC Prize X posts (Opus 5.5 $0.41/$0.16; Fable 5.1 $3.12/$1.40) returned HTTP 402; the Opus 5.5 results page carries no cost figures, so Opus 5.5 per-task ARC cost is X-post-only and the Fable 5.1 $3.12 vs $4.49 discrepancy stays unresolved.
- No third-party measurement (tokens, minutes, pass rate, bugs found) of Claude Code ultracode, dynamic workflows, agent teams or managed Code Review on Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1; ClawArena-Team, the closest benchmark, tests Fable 5 and Sonnet 4.6.
- No per-effort multi-agent evidence at any level for either model; all effort-level evidence is single-agent (ARC Prize, Artificial Analysis, Vals, Scale HLE, Arena.ai, CodeRabbit, Firecrawl).
- Artificial Analysis launch-article numbers (Fable 5.1 max 66, 143.7M tokens, $3.76) and current model-page numbers (max 53, 190M tokens, $7.63; xhigh 53; medium 49) disagree, apparently across index versions; per-level Fable 5.1 'high' page numbers were not retrieved.
- CodeRabbit's 'Max' and 'Standard' are internal configurations described only as higher/lower reasoning-effort settings; they cannot be mapped to a named Anthropic effort level.
- No METR time-horizon value for Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1; METR's research index lists no Fable 5.1 evaluation; no METR best-of-k or scaffold result.
- Terminal-Bench 4.0 public leaderboard (tbench.ai / Harbor hub) rows could not be extracted by fetch; the claimed absence of an Opus 5.5 row rests on a search snippet.
- Scale SEAL SWE-bench Pro public board lists no Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1 row; BenchLM's 89.9% / 81.2% figures have no stated provenance. Scale HLE lists Fable 5.1 (xhigh) but no Opus 5.5. Arena.ai lists Fable 5.1 (Max) but no Opus 5.5.
- Epoch AI shows Fable 5.1 FrontierMath 90% / 88% and Opus 5.5 EBR-bench 71% without effort settings.
- morphllm agent-teams page (7x plan mode, 3-5x) returned HTTP 429 on every fetch; figures remain snippet-only.
- Cross-model review, Adversarial Review and DeLM use Opus 4.7, Sonnet 4.5 and Opus 4.6 respectively; no equivalent study on Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1 found.
- Web-search budget was exhausted mid-task (200/200), so LMArena text/webdev, Aider, LiveCodeBench, ultrareview and additional practitioner ultracode reports for these two models were not searched further.
- Anthropic docs state that with ultracode on, the concurrent-subagent limit is not enforced and the Large-workflow warning is suppressed by design; GitHub issue #92311 documents the same behavior as a bug. No independent measurement of resulting token cost.

**Gaps**

- The only per-level measurement spanning all five efforts for both models (kaybenleroll, 25 Sep 2026) is a single synthetic tool-less plan-review document with 13 seeded defects, n=10 (Opus) or n=5 (Fable, different prompt), unadjusted for 16 contrasts; it is not a code-diff or security review and cannot be generalised.
- No third-party study measures code-diff review recall/precision for Opus 5.5 at pure API low, medium, xhigh or max; CodeRabbit's 'Standard'/'Max' are pipeline configurations, and the only max data at API level is one n=1 run.
- No third-party code-diff review or security-audit measurement exists for Fable 5.1 at medium, xhigh or max; CodeRabbit tested only low and high, and AA shows xhigh and max tie at index 53.
- Whether Fable 5.1 honours the effort parameter at all is unresolved: a Claude Code 2.1.260 changelog fixed a client bug that ignored frontmatter effort on pinned-default-effort models including Fable 5, and the planned Fable effort test (issue #121) has no results.
- depthfirst publishes no per-model per-effort dfbench table in text; the Opus 5.5 high figure (54.9%/48.5%/$8.42) exists only via StationX's relay of a depthfirst employee, and depthfirst's own page says macro recall declines at higher thinking modes.
- Qodo's own blog page contains Claude's numbers only in chart images; the 48% recall / 60.8 F1 quoted by the original researcher could not be found anywhere and is treated as unsupported; the 52% / 62.7 figures come from Qodo's dev.to post and X account.
- No CyberGym, CyberGym-E2E, SWE-bench Pro (Scale SEAL), Epoch SWE-bench Verified or CVE-bench scores were found for Opus 5.5, Opus 5, Fable 5.1 or Fable 5; leaderboards stop at Opus 4.6/4.7 or Mythos.
- The only 11,000-line-scale review datapoint (mejba.me) omits the verified bug count, false positives and model version, and Anthropic's ultrareview now caps branch reviews at 8,000 changed lines / 500 files by default, so an 11,000-line review would be refused without splitting.
- No study measures miss rate versus diff size for Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1; the only diff-size curve (F1 0.657 to 0.043) is for Sonnet 4.6 / Haiku 4.5, and the contrary 84%-vs-31% figure is Anthropic internal data for its managed Code Review, not ultrareview.
- No third-party measurement of second-pass or multi-agent review value names Opus 5.5 or Fable 5.1; the cross-model paper used Opus 4.7 and GPT-5.5 at high only; SWR-Bench aggregation used Gemini.
- Both models route some security work to Opus 4.8 via cyber classifiers (binary vulnerability discovery, exploitation, pentest planning); no source quantifies how often this fires during an offline source-code review, and the ~60% reduction figure is Anthropic's.
- Artificial Analysis index versions differ: Fable 5.1's 58-66 ladder (older revision) vs v4.3.2's 47-53; only v4.3.2 numbers (Opus 5.5 42-58, Fable 5.1 47-53) are comparable.
- Web search budget was exhausted during this pass; Vals Terminal-Bench 2.1 page (404), tbench.ai and Epoch SWE-bench Verified pages returned no rows to the fetcher, so LMArena, ARC-AGI, HLE and Terminal-Bench leaderboard per-effort entries for either model remain unchecked.
- No third-party token or dollar cost is published for a full code-review or security review of a multi-thousand-line diff at each effort level for either model; the closest are kaybenleroll's $0.34-$2.21 per single-document run and CodeRabbit's percentage token deltas.

**Gaps**

- No third-party measurement of Claude Code ultracode, Agent Teams or multi-agent review that includes Opus 5.5 or compares it with Fable 5.1. All multi-agent numbers are Fable 5.1-only (MindStudio, paddo), Opus 5 (vibecodingacademy), Opus 4.8/4.6 anecdotes, or invented-token worked examples (orcarouter). The web-search budget was exhausted before additional searches for this cell could run.
- No hands-on coding-session cost for Opus 5.5 at low, xhigh or max, or for Fable 5.1 at medium or xhigh; those cells rest on Artificial Analysis and ARC Prize only (plus AA per-benchmark Elo/score pages now added).
- Refuted parts of the original set: the ARC Prize x.com post could not be fetched (402), so the ARC-AGI-1 $0.16/task and '80% lower cost' figures are unverified; christopheralarcon does not mention vulnerability discovery; aifreeapi does not say Opus 5.5 has no ceiling; VentureBeat does not carry the FrontierCode 54.6% medium or HLE-with-tools 67.7%/65.6% figures; benchlm SWE-bench Pro shows no 'Vendor-reported' label; the '9 of 11 components' count is not on the AA comparison page.
- Attribution corrections: kingy.ai CursorBench costs come from Cursor's evals table, not Anthropic; CodeRabbit's 'Standard'/'Max' are pipeline configurations, not API effort levels; wmedia.es ran Fable 5.1 and Opus 5 at high (Claude Code default), so its Fable figures belong to the high cell; paddo.dev's 96%-at-46% figure is Anthropic's; rohitghumare and orcarouter are explicitly non-measured worked examples.
- Vals.ai inconsistencies: Opus 5.5 page says '#1 of 63' (researcher wrote 65); Fable 5.1 page shows both 67.87% (#1, prose) and 68.83% (rank 2/65, table); the 'Terminal-Bench 2.1 at high effort' note and the 'below Opus 5 on MedCode and SAGE' comparison were not found on the pages.
- Artificial Analysis figures exist in two incompatible index versions (1 Sept 2026 Fable 5.1 launch article: 58-66, max $3.76, xhigh $2.72; current v4.3.2: 47-53, max $7.63, xhigh $5.98). Do not mix launch-article Fable 5.1 figures with current Opus 5.5 figures. AA article gives Opus 5.5 Terminal-Bench 4.0 as 59.6%; comparison page rounds to 60%.
- Snorkel and Endor Labs state no effort level for either model; Endor also ran different Claude Code versions per model (2.1.280 vs 2.1.258), so pass/cost differences are partly harness-confounded.
- Leaderboards that did not render or returned 404 through the fetch tool: tbench.ai Terminal-Bench 4.0 rows, Epoch ECI table, LiveCodeBench, swebench.com Verified table, ARC Prize leaderboard tables, AA terminal-bench and automationbench evaluation URLs, Vals terminal-bench. No independent SWE-bench Verified/Pro, Epoch ECI or LiveCodeBench (beyond Vals' 90.52% Fable 5.1 max) figure for Opus 5.5 was read.
- METR publishes no time horizon for any Claude 5-series model (time-horizons page tops out at Claude Mythos Preview, 8 May 2026); Scale SEAL/SWE-bench Pro public board has no 5-series Claude entries; Aider unmaintained since 20 Nov 2025.
- Arena text leaderboard shows only one effort variant per model (opus-5.5-high, fable-5.1-max) with overlapping confidence intervals and a small Opus 5.5 vote count (2,307), so it does not support a per-level comparison.
- Anthropic-hosted or Anthropic-sourced material used as secondary only: claude.dev cost post (Addy Osmani), VentureBeat launch figures, DataCamp SWE-bench numbers, laozhang and paddo quoted Anthropic ratios.
